# Codex audit — Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (CollabSession transport wrappers) 2026-05-19

## Context

Working dir: `/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook` (engine worktree).
HEAD: `ffd8f6e5f05` (Phase 5.5 V2 V1). Predecessor: `bfa8401e1b5` (5.5 V1 audit closures).

## What shipped

Single commit adding typed transport methods on `CollabSession`. Workspace tests 4196 → 4205 (+9).

Changes to `crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs`:
1. `CollabSession` adds `transport: Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>` field.
2. New `CollabSessionError::Transport(#[from] TransportError)` variant.
3. 5 new methods:
   - `attach_transport<T: Transport + Send + 'static>(&mut self, t: T) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>` — wraps in Box, returns prior if any.
   - `detach_transport(&mut self) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>` — takes the transport.
   - `has_transport(&self) -> bool`.
   - `flush_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError>` — `Ok(false)` if no transport; else exports current snapshot via `OpLog::export_bytes()` and pushes via `Transport::send`.
   - `poll_remote(&mut self) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError>` — `Ok(0)` if no transport; else drains via `while let Some(bytes) = transport.try_recv()? { self.log.merge_bytes(&bytes)?; }` and returns merge count.
4. `Debug` impl extended with `transport_attached: bool`.

9 new tests cover: attach returning None / prior; detach returning Some then None; flush/poll no-op without attached; flush pushes verified via pending_recv; poll drains pre-populated queue; full 2-session round-trip via attached LoopbackTransport pair; Debug reflects attach state.

## What you need to verify

### A. Soundness

A1. `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` requires `Transport` to be object-safe AND impls to be `Send`. Verify: Transport trait at `transport.rs:54-76` is object-safe (`&mut self` methods, primitive params); NoopTransport + LoopbackTransport are Send (no !Send fields). Pin via static assertion?

A2. The `flush_to_transport` exports the FULL snapshot every call. For an editing session with thousands of ops, this is O(state). Is this acceptable for V2 V1, or does V1 need a delta-export mechanism right away?

A3. `flush_to_transport` returns `Ok(false)` when no transport. `Result<bool>` is awkward for a "tristate" (no-transport / sent / error). Consider `Ok(())` + `has_transport()` check, OR a dedicated `Outcome` enum.

A4. `poll_remote` drains in a `while let` loop. If the transport's recv-queue is backed by an external source that's constantly producing, this could spin forever. Should V1 cap the drain at N blobs per call?

A5. Error semantics: `flush_to_transport` returns `Err` on transport.send failure. The local log is unaffected (`export_bytes` ran first, send was the last step). Caller can retry. Documented?

### B. API design

B1. `attach_transport<T: Transport + Send + 'static>` requires `'static` because of `Box<dyn Trait + Send>`. Too restrictive? Acceptable since transports own their state.

B2. `detach_transport` returns `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` — caller can't downcast back to the concrete type without `Any` machinery. Acceptable for V1?

B3. `attach_transport` returning the previous boxed transport (or `None`) is a swap-semantic. Should there be a separate `set_transport` (panic if already attached) for stricter use cases?

B4. Per the trait contract, `try_recv` returns `Closed` only after queue is empty. `poll_remote` propagates this as `CollabSessionError::Transport(TransportError::Closed)`. Caller can detach + reattach. Test coverage gap?

### C. Test coverage

C1. The 9 new tests cover: attach/detach/has, flush no-op, poll no-op, flush pushes, poll drains, 2-session convergence, Debug. Anything missing?

C2. No test for `flush_to_transport` returning `Err` (transport.send fails). Easy to test by attaching a transport that's been closed beforehand.

C3. No test for `poll_remote` returning `Err` (transport.try_recv fails with Io/Closed mid-drain). Mid-drain Closed is interesting: should `poll_remote` return the count of successful merges before the error?

C4. The 2-session convergence test uses `op_count() == op_count()` as convergence check — but Loro's snapshot import could merge to different op counts depending on which peer's snapshot is freshest. Verify the test actually asserts something meaningful.

C5. No test for "attach + flush + detach + reattach" cycle.

### D. Doc accuracy

D1. `crdt-data-model.md` § Transport (V1 partially shipped) — does V2 V1 need updating?

D2. `entry-plan.md` 5.5 row currently says "V1 SHIPPED `924750819bc`" — needs V2 V1 marker?

D3. `known-gaps.md` GAP-C-05 (partial) — needs updating to reflect attached-transport API?

D4. `transport.rs` module docstring mentions V1 / V2 — needs V2 V1 mention?

D5. `session.rs` module docstring scaffold-overview Phase 5.5 bullet — needs V2 V1 marker?

### E. Gates

E1. `cargo test --workspace` — expect 4205.
E2. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` — expect clean.
E3. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` — expect clean.

## Output format

A1-A5 / B1-B4 / C1-C5 / D1-D5 / E1-E3 with verdicts. Findings table HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW + file:line + concrete fixes. End with "Phase 5.5 V2 V1 is ship-clean" / "...N MUST-FIX before next session" / "...N SHOULD-FIX for cleanup". Be specific.

 succeeded in 309ms:
diff --git a/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs b/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs
index 61861dbdf90..173ceb61052 100644
--- a/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs
+++ b/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs
@@ -1,403 +1,505 @@
 //! `CollabSession` — per-peer collaboration state holder.
 //!
 //! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** wraps `ql_oplog::OpLog`
 //! with peer-id-aware operations + transport plumbing. Subsequent
 //! phases populate richer behavior:
 //!
 //! - ~~**Phase 5.4**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `89c02b9d83e` — per-peer
 //!   Loro `UndoManager` tracks local appends. Undo APPENDS
 //!   inverse ops + retracts originals from the visible `"ops"`
 //!   list (NOT physical removal). Presence-origin commits
 //!   excluded. See [`session::CollabSession::undo`].
 //! - **Phase 5.5** — transport layer. V1 shipped at
 //!   `924750819bc` (LoopbackTransport for in-process 2-peer
 //!   tests); V2 pending — WebSocket impl + auto-flush
 //!   (append a local op → broadcast bytes via
 //!   [`crate::Transport::send`]; poll
 //!   [`crate::Transport::try_recv`] for remote bytes →
 //!   `merge_bytes`).
 //! - ~~**Phase 5.6**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `c677e244704` — `presence`
 //!   module + 4 `CollabSession` methods (`update_presence` /
 //!   `peer_presence` / `clear_presence` / `peers_with_presence`).
 //! - **Phase 5.7** — IDE binding: `CollabSession` becomes the engine
 //!   handle that the IDE attaches to each open workbook.
 //!
 //! What ships in 5.2.a (5.2.b update 2026-05-19):
 //!
 //! - `CollabSession::new(peer_id)` — fresh session with an empty
 //!   `OpLog` and the peer-id wired through to
 //!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` (Phase 5.2.b — was a label-only stamp
 //!   in 5.2.a). Returns `Result` because Loro's `set_peer_id` is
 //!   fallible.
 //! - `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` — fork from a
 //!   shared base snapshot (used by both peers in the D-4 probe
 //!   pattern). The reborn session's peer id is `peer_id`; existing
 //!   ops in the imported snapshot retain their original
 //!   attribution.
 //! - `append_op(&mut self, Op)` — local append; delegates to
 //!   `OpLog::append`.
 //! - `merge_bytes(&mut self, &[u8])` — pull a remote peer's
 //!   snapshot via Loro's CRDT merge; delegates to
 //!   `OpLog::merge_bytes`.
 //! - `export_bytes(&self)` — produce a snapshot for transport.
 //! - `op_log(&self)` — read-only view of the underlying log
 //!   (for replay against a workbook).
 //! - `peer_id(&self)` — the session's stable peer id.
 
 use thiserror::Error;
 
 use ql_oplog::{Op, OpLog, OpLogError, PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN};
 
 use crate::peer::PeerId;
 use crate::presence::{self, PresenceError, PresenceState};
+use crate::transport::{Transport, TransportError};
 
 /// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
 #[derive(Debug, Error)]
 #[non_exhaustive]
 pub enum CollabSessionError {
     /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
     /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
     #[error("op log error: {0}")]
     OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),
 
     /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
     /// underlying OpLog).
     #[error("presence error: {0}")]
     Presence(#[from] PresenceError),
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
     /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
     /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
     /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
     #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
     Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),
+
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 (2026-05-19):** the attached `Transport`
+    /// returned an error during `flush_to_transport` or
+    /// `poll_remote`. The local `OpLog` state is unaffected — the
+    /// caller can retry the flush/poll or detach the transport.
+    #[error("transport error: {0}")]
+    Transport(#[from] TransportError),
 }
 
 /// Per-peer collaboration state holder.
 ///
 /// Owns an `OpLog` + a stable `PeerId`. Tested with `NoopTransport`
 /// at scaffold time; Phase 5.5 will add the live transport
 /// integration via `Transport::send` / `try_recv`.
 ///
 /// The session is the engine-side handle for one peer's view of a
 /// shared workbook. Multiple peers each hold their own
 /// `CollabSession`, exchange byte blobs via [`crate::Transport`],
 /// and call `merge_bytes` to incorporate remote ops. All sessions
 /// converge to the same `OpLog` content after exchange (per Loro's
 /// CRDT determinism — Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1).
 pub struct CollabSession {
     peer_id: PeerId,
     log: OpLog,
     /// Phase 5.4 V1 — peer-local undo/redo.
     ///
     /// Drop-order note (Codex 5.4 V1 audit LOW): Rust drops fields
     /// in DECLARATION order, so this drops LAST (after `log`).
     /// That is safe here because `loro::UndoManager` owns its own
     /// `LoroDoc` clone (`loro-internal::undo:166,670`) — it doesn't
     /// borrow from `log`'s doc and won't UAF when `log` drops first.
     undo: loro::UndoManager,
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** optional attached
+    /// transport. `None` until `attach_transport` is called.
+    /// Boxed + `Send` so callers can move sessions between
+    /// threads with attached transports.
+    ///
+    /// V2 V1 scope: caller drives flush + poll explicitly
+    /// (no auto-flush on append). V2 V2 / V3 will add
+    /// auto-flush + version-vector delta exports.
+    transport: Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>,
 }
 
 impl CollabSession {
     /// Construct a fresh session with an empty op log.
     ///
     /// Use this when no shared base snapshot exists yet (the first
     /// peer to open a brand-new workbook). The `peer_id` is wired
     /// through to the underlying `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so Loro's
     /// CRDT merge metadata attributes this session's appends to
     /// `peer_id` (not Loro's default random per-doc id).
     ///
     /// **Caller pitfalls** (from `OpLog::set_peer_id`):
     /// - Two concurrent sessions MUST use distinct peer ids.
     ///   Duplicate ids corrupt the document via conflicting OpIDs.
     /// - Prefer per-process-random peer ids over user-/device-pinned
     ///   ids unless your transport layer enforces single-ownership.
     /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is a Loro-reserved sentinel and returns
     ///   `CollabSessionError::OpLog`.
     pub fn new(peer_id: PeerId) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
         let mut log = OpLog::new();
         log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
         let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
-        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
+        Ok(Self {
+            peer_id,
+            log,
+            undo,
+            transport: None,
+        })
     }
 
     /// Construct a session by importing a shared snapshot. Use this
     /// when joining a session that already has history (the second+
     /// peer to open a collaborative workbook).
     ///
     /// `bytes` is a `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Snapshot)` produced
     /// by an earlier `CollabSession::export_bytes` call (typically
     /// from the workbook owner / first peer). After import, this
     /// session's peer id is set to `peer_id` — distinct from the
     /// peer ids carried by the already-imported ops. Imported ops
     /// retain their original peer-id attribution (Loro semantics);
     /// only this session's future appends carry `peer_id`.
     ///
     /// **Caller pitfalls** (same as `CollabSession::new`):
     /// - The new `peer_id` MUST differ from every peer already
     ///   represented in the imported snapshot AND from every other
     ///   concurrent session — see `OpLog::set_peer_id` for details.
     /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is reserved.
     pub fn from_snapshot(peer_id: PeerId, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
         let mut log = OpLog::import_bytes(bytes)?;
         log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
         let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
-        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
+        Ok(Self {
+            peer_id,
+            log,
+            undo,
+            transport: None,
+        })
     }
 
     /// Stable peer id assigned at session creation.
     pub fn peer_id(&self) -> PeerId {
         self.peer_id
     }
 
     /// Append a local op. Delegates to `OpLog::append`. Phase 5.5
     /// will additionally push the appended bytes through the
     /// attached `Transport`; 5.2.a leaves transport integration to
     /// the caller.
     pub fn append_op(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
         self.log.append(op)?;
         Ok(())
     }
 
     /// Merge a remote peer's snapshot into this session's log. Loro's
     /// CRDT preserves both peers' concurrent appends in deterministic
     /// causal order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
     /// Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1). Returns the new `len()` after merge.
     pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
         Ok(self.log.merge_bytes(bytes)?)
     }
 
     /// Produce a Loro snapshot blob suitable for transport to other
     /// peers OR for `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence.
     pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CollabSessionError> {
         Ok(self.log.export_bytes()?)
     }
 
     /// Borrow the underlying `OpLog` for replay against a `Workbook`.
     ///
     /// Typical usage:
     /// ```ignore
     /// let session = CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, &bytes)?;
     /// let mut wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
     /// let reg = ql_functions::default_registry();
     /// ql_oplog::replay_into(session.op_log(), &mut wb, &reg)?;
     /// ```
     pub fn op_log(&self) -> &OpLog {
         &self.log
     }
 
     /// Current number of ops in the log. Cheap (cached).
     pub fn op_count(&self) -> usize {
         self.log.len()
     }
 
     /// True iff the log has no ops (session just opened, no edits yet).
     pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
         self.log.is_empty()
     }
 
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** attach a `Transport`
+    /// implementation to this session. Subsequent
+    /// [`flush_to_transport`] calls push the session's current
+    /// snapshot through it; [`poll_remote`] drains incoming
+    /// bytes and merges them.
+    ///
+    /// Returns `Some(previous)` if a transport was already
+    /// attached and got replaced. V2 V1 does NOT auto-flush on
+    /// `append_op` — caller drives flush + poll explicitly. V3
+    /// will add auto-flush + version-vector delta exports.
+    pub fn attach_transport<T: Transport + Send + 'static>(
+        &mut self,
+        transport: T,
+    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
+        self.transport.replace(Box::new(transport))
+    }
+
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** detach the current
+    /// transport. Returns it for caller cleanup; returns `None`
+    /// if no transport was attached.
+    pub fn detach_transport(&mut self) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
+        self.transport.take()
+    }
+
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff a transport is
+    /// currently attached.
+    pub fn has_transport(&self) -> bool {
+        self.transport.is_some()
+    }
+
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** export the current
+    /// session snapshot and push it through the attached
+    /// transport. No-op (returns `Ok(false)`) if no transport
+    /// attached.
+    ///
+    /// Returns `Ok(true)` if bytes were sent. Errors propagate
+    /// from either the underlying `OpLog::export_bytes` or the
+    /// transport's `send`. The local `OpLog` is unaffected by
+    /// transport errors — caller can retry the flush.
+    ///
+    /// V2 V1 sends the FULL snapshot each call (O(state)). V3
+    /// will track per-transport version vectors and send deltas
+    /// only.
+    pub fn flush_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
+        let Some(transport) = self.transport.as_mut() else {
+            return Ok(false);
+        };
+        let bytes = self.log.export_bytes()?;
+        transport.send(&bytes)?;
+        Ok(true)
+    }
+
+    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** drain the attached
+    /// transport's recv queue, merging each blob into the local
+    /// `OpLog`. Returns the number of blobs merged.
+    ///
+    /// Returns `Ok(0)` if no transport attached. Stops draining
+    /// at the first `try_recv` returning `None` (queue empty);
+    /// propagates `Closed` / `Io` errors as
+    /// `CollabSessionError::Transport`. Per the trait contract,
+    /// any already-queued bytes are drained BEFORE `Closed`
+    /// surfaces.
+    pub fn poll_remote(&mut self) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
+        let Some(transport) = self.transport.as_mut() else {
+            return Ok(0);
+        };
+        let mut merged = 0usize;
+        while let Some(bytes) = transport.try_recv()? {
+            self.log.merge_bytes(&bytes)?;
+            merged += 1;
+        }
+        Ok(merged)
+    }
+
     /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** write this session's own
     /// presence state into the shared `"presence"` LoroMap.
     ///
     /// Uses the session's `PeerId` as the map key (16-hex
     /// `Display` form). Subsequent calls overwrite the previous
     /// value (LWW per peer); merges with other peers'
     /// presence writes preserve all distinct peers.
     pub fn update_presence(&mut self, state: PresenceState) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
         let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
         let json = serde_json::to_string(&state).map_err(PresenceError::Serialize)?;
         self.log.presence_set(&key, &json)?;
         Ok(())
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** read a peer's most recent
     /// presence state. Returns `Ok(None)` if the peer has never
     /// updated its presence in this session (or has been removed
     /// via `clear_presence`).
     pub fn peer_presence(&self, peer: PeerId) -> Result<Option<PresenceState>, CollabSessionError> {
         let key = presence::peer_key(peer);
         let Some(json) = self.log.presence_get(&key)? else {
             return Ok(None);
         };
         let state: PresenceState =
             serde_json::from_str(&json).map_err(|source| PresenceError::Deserialize {
                 peer_key: key,
                 source,
             })?;
         Ok(Some(state))
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** remove this session's own
     /// presence entry from the shared map. Use this when the
     /// peer leaves the session (window close, disconnect). After
     /// removal, other peers' `peer_presence(self_id)` returns
     /// `Ok(None)`.
     pub fn clear_presence(&mut self) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
         let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
         self.log.presence_remove(&key)?;
         Ok(())
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** undo this session's last
     /// local op. Loro's `UndoManager` semantically inverts it by
     /// appending an inverse op (NOT by physical removal). Returns
     /// `Ok(true)` if an undo stack item was consumed,
     /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
     ///
     /// Local-only per Loro's `UndoManager` contract — remote ops
     /// from other peers (merged via `merge_bytes`) are NOT
     /// affected.
     ///
     /// Presence updates are excluded from the undo stack by
     /// construction (see `CollabSession::new`), so cursor movements
     /// don't consume undo slots.
     pub fn undo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
         Ok(self.undo.undo()?)
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** redo the last undone op.
     /// Returns `Ok(true)` if a redo stack item was consumed,
     /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
     pub fn redo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
         Ok(self.undo.redo()?)
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the undo stack has
     /// at least one item.
     pub fn can_undo(&self) -> bool {
         self.undo.can_undo()
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the redo stack has
     /// at least one item.
     pub fn can_redo(&self) -> bool {
         self.undo.can_redo()
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
     /// on the undo stack.
     pub fn undo_count(&self) -> usize {
         self.undo.undo_count()
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
     /// on the redo stack.
     pub fn redo_count(&self) -> usize {
         self.undo.redo_count()
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** clear both undo and redo
     /// stacks. Use when starting a fresh logical session (e.g.
     /// opening a new workbook tab while reusing the
     /// `CollabSession` shell).
     pub fn clear_undo_stack(&self) {
         self.undo.clear();
     }
 
     /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** list every peer with a
     /// presence entry in the shared map.
     ///
     /// Order is Loro's iteration order. Sort the result if you
     /// need determinism.
     ///
     /// Returns `Err(CollabSessionError::Presence(PresenceError::PeerKeyParse))`
     /// if a key in the map can't be parsed as a `PeerId` (only
     /// reachable if a future writer uses an incompatible
     /// encoding).
     pub fn peers_with_presence(&self) -> Result<Vec<PeerId>, CollabSessionError> {
         let keys = self.log.presence_peers();
         let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(keys.len());
         for k in keys {
             out.push(presence::parse_peer_key(&k)?);
         }
         Ok(out)
     }
 }
 
 impl std::fmt::Debug for CollabSession {
     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
         f.debug_struct("CollabSession")
             .field("peer_id", &self.peer_id)
             .field("op_count", &self.log.len())
             .field("undo_count", &self.undo.undo_count())
             .field("redo_count", &self.undo.redo_count())
+            .field("transport_attached", &self.transport.is_some())
             .finish()
     }
 }
 
 /// Construct + configure a Loro `UndoManager` bound to `log`'s
 /// underlying doc.
 ///
 /// Phase 5.4 V1 setup: register `PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN` as an
 /// exclude prefix so cursor-movement commits don't fill the
 /// undo stack. (Phase 5.6 V1 tags presence writes with that
 /// origin via `OpLog::presence_set` / `presence_remove`.)
 fn make_undo_manager(log: &OpLog) -> loro::UndoManager {
     let mut undo = log.new_undo_manager();
     undo.add_exclude_origin_prefix(PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN);
     undo
 }

 mod tests {
     use super::*;
     use ql_oplog::CellWireValue;
 
     fn put_value(sheet: u16, row: u32, col: u32, n: f64) -> Op {
         Op::PutValue {
             sheet,
             row,
             col,
             value: CellWireValue::Number(n),
         }
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn new_session_is_empty() {
         let s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(s.peer_id(), PeerId::new(1));
         assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 0);
         assert!(s.is_empty());
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn append_grows_log() {
         let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         s.append_op(Op::AddSheet {
             name: "S".to_owned(),
             chunk_rows: 16384,
         })
         .unwrap();
         s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 42.0)).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 2);
         assert!(!s.is_empty());
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn from_snapshot_reconstructs_op_count() {
         let mut origin = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         origin
             .append_op(Op::AddSheet {
                 name: "S".to_owned(),
                 chunk_rows: 16384,
             })
             .unwrap();
         origin.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0)).unwrap();
 
         let bytes = origin.export_bytes().unwrap();
         let reborn = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(2), &bytes).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(reborn.peer_id(), PeerId::new(2));
         assert_eq!(reborn.op_count(), 2);
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn merge_bytes_grows_log_with_remote_ops() {
         // Two peers, shared base, each appends; one merges the other.
         let mut base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(100)).unwrap();
         base.append_op(Op::AddSheet {
             name: "S".to_owned(),
             chunk_rows: 16384,
         })
         .unwrap();
         let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
 
@@ -706,160 +808,297 @@ mod tests {
         s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 1, 2.0)).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 2);
         assert_eq!(s.op_log().iter().count(), 2);
 
         assert!(s.undo().unwrap());
         // op_count + iter().count() must agree post-undo.
         assert_eq!(s.op_log().iter().count(), s.op_count());
         assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 1, "visible op count must shrink to 1");
 
         // Redo restores.
         assert!(s.redo().unwrap());
         assert_eq!(s.op_log().iter().count(), s.op_count());
         assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 2);
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn reborn_session_has_empty_undo_stack() {
         // Codex 5.4 V1 audit C3 closure: imported ops are NOT
         // undoable by the reborn peer (Loro local-only undo
         // semantics — `loro-internal::undo:615-643` composes
         // imported events into `remote_event` rather than pushing
         // them onto the undo stack).
         let mut origin = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(11)).unwrap();
         origin.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0)).unwrap();
         origin.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 1, 2.0)).unwrap();
         assert!(origin.can_undo());
         let bytes = origin.export_bytes().unwrap();
 
         let reborn = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(22), &bytes).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(
             reborn.undo_count(),
             0,
             "reborn session must NOT inherit origin's undo stack"
         );
         assert!(!reborn.can_undo());
         // But it sees the imported ops in the log.
         assert_eq!(reborn.op_count(), 2);
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn local_undo_after_remote_merge_preserves_remote_ops() {
         // Codex 5.4 V1 audit C2 closure: peer A appends, B merges A's
         // bytes, A undoes A's append, B re-merges. After re-merge, B
         // sees A's op as retracted (Loro propagates the undo's inverse
         // through the CRDT). The remote contribution from B's own
         // appends survives unchanged.
         let base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
 
         let mut peer_a = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xa1), &base_bytes).unwrap();
         peer_a.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 10.0)).unwrap();
 
         let mut peer_b = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xb1), &base_bytes).unwrap();
         peer_b.append_op(put_value(0, 1, 0, 20.0)).unwrap();
         peer_b.merge_bytes(&peer_a.export_bytes().unwrap()).unwrap();
         let b_count_before = peer_b.op_count();
         assert!(b_count_before >= 2, "B sees A's + B's ops");
 
         // A undoes its append. iter().count() shrinks on A.
         assert!(peer_a.undo().unwrap());
         let a_iter = peer_a.op_log().iter().count();
         assert!(
             a_iter < 1 || a_iter == 0,
             "A's visible ops shrink post-undo, got {a_iter}"
         );
 
         // B merges A's post-undo state. A's retract should propagate.
         peer_b.merge_bytes(&peer_a.export_bytes().unwrap()).unwrap();
         let b_iter_after = peer_b.op_log().iter().count();
         assert!(
             b_iter_after < b_count_before,
             "B's visible op count must shrink after merging A's undo (was {b_count_before}, now {b_iter_after})"
         );
         // But B still sees its OWN op — undo is local-only.
         assert!(
             b_iter_after >= 1,
             "B's own op must survive A's local undo (local-only semantics)"
         );
     }
 
+    #[test]
+    fn attach_transport_returns_none_when_no_prior() {
+        use crate::transport::NoopTransport;
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        assert!(!s.has_transport());
+        let prior = s.attach_transport(NoopTransport::new());
+        assert!(prior.is_none());
+        assert!(s.has_transport());
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn attach_transport_returns_previous_on_replace() {
+        use crate::transport::NoopTransport;
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        s.attach_transport(NoopTransport::new());
+        let prior = s.attach_transport(NoopTransport::new());
+        assert!(prior.is_some(), "second attach must return the first");

+    #[test]
+    fn detach_transport_returns_box_then_clears() {
+        use crate::transport::NoopTransport;
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        s.attach_transport(NoopTransport::new());
+        assert!(s.has_transport());
+        let detached = s.detach_transport();
+        assert!(detached.is_some());
+        assert!(!s.has_transport());
+        assert!(s.detach_transport().is_none(), "second detach is None");
+    }

+    #[test]
+    fn flush_to_transport_noop_without_attached() {
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        let flushed = s.flush_to_transport().unwrap();
+        assert!(!flushed, "flush with no transport returns false");
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn poll_remote_noop_without_attached() {
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        let drained = s.poll_remote().unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(drained, 0, "poll with no transport returns 0");
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn flush_to_transport_pushes_bytes_via_attached() {
+        use crate::transport::LoopbackTransport;
+        let (tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 42.0)).unwrap();
+        s.attach_transport(tx_a);
+
+        assert_eq!(
+            tx_b.pending_recv(),
+            0,
+            "transport idle until flush is called"
+        );
+        let flushed = s.flush_to_transport().unwrap();
+        assert!(flushed);
+        assert_eq!(tx_b.pending_recv(), 1, "flush pushed exactly one blob");
+
+        // Sanity: the pushed bytes are a valid Loro snapshot
+        // (poll on B's side drains them).
+        let received = tx_b.try_recv().unwrap().expect("tx_b drains one blob");
+        assert!(!received.is_empty());
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn poll_remote_drains_attached_transport() {
+        use crate::transport::LoopbackTransport;
+        let (tx_a, tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
+        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        // Pre-populate tx_a's inbox by writing into tx_b's outbox via
+        // tx_b's send. (tx_b.send → tx_a.inbox)
+        let snapshot = {
+            let other = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(2)).unwrap();
+            other.export_bytes().unwrap()
+        };
+        let mut tx_b = tx_b; // own it for send
+        tx_b.send(&snapshot).unwrap();
+        tx_b.send(&snapshot).unwrap();
+
+        s.attach_transport(tx_a);
+        let drained = s.poll_remote().unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(drained, 2, "poll drained both queued blobs");
+
+        // Next poll is a no-op (queue empty).
+        let drained2 = s.poll_remote().unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(drained2, 0);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn two_sessions_converge_via_attached_loopback() {
+        use crate::transport::LoopbackTransport;
+        let (tx_a, tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
+
+        let base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
+        let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
+
+        let mut peer_a = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xa), &base_bytes).unwrap();
+        peer_a.attach_transport(tx_a);
+        let mut peer_b = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xb), &base_bytes).unwrap();
+        peer_b.attach_transport(tx_b);
+
+        // A appends + flushes; B polls.
+        peer_a.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 10.0)).unwrap();
+        peer_a.flush_to_transport().unwrap();
+        let drained_b = peer_b.poll_remote().unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(drained_b, 1);
+        assert!(peer_b.op_count() >= peer_a.op_count());
+
+        // Reverse: B appends + flushes; A polls.
+        peer_b.append_op(put_value(0, 1, 0, 20.0)).unwrap();
+        peer_b.flush_to_transport().unwrap();
+        let drained_a = peer_a.poll_remote().unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(drained_a, 1);
+        assert_eq!(peer_a.op_count(), peer_b.op_count());
+    }

+        let d_post = format!("{s:?}");
+        assert!(
+            d_post.contains("transport_attached: true"),

     #[test]
     fn two_sessions_exchange_state_via_loopback_transport() {
         // Phase 5.5 V1 integration: drive two CollabSessions
         // through a LoopbackTransport pair. V1 doesn't auto-
         // flush appends to the transport (5.5 V2 work), so the
         // test manually exports/sends/recvs/merges to verify the
         // wire actually carries the right bytes.
         use crate::transport::{LoopbackTransport, Transport};
         let (mut tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
 
         let base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
 
         let mut peer_a = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xa), &base_bytes).unwrap();
         let mut peer_b = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xb), &base_bytes).unwrap();
 
         // Peer A appends an op, exports, sends over the wire.
         peer_a.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 42.0)).unwrap();
         let a_bytes = peer_a.export_bytes().unwrap();
         tx_a.send(&a_bytes).unwrap();
 
         // Peer B drains the transport and merges.
         let received = tx_b
             .try_recv()
             .unwrap()
             .expect("tx_b must receive A's bytes");
         assert_eq!(received, a_bytes);
         peer_b.merge_bytes(&received).unwrap();
         assert!(peer_b.op_count() >= 1, "B must see A's op after merge");
 
         // Reverse direction: B appends, sends to A.
         peer_b.append_op(put_value(0, 1, 0, 99.0)).unwrap();
         tx_b.send(&peer_b.export_bytes().unwrap()).unwrap();
         let b_received = tx_a
             .try_recv()
             .unwrap()
             .expect("tx_a must receive B's bytes");
         peer_a.merge_bytes(&b_received).unwrap();
 
         // After the exchange, both sessions converge on the same op count.
         assert_eq!(peer_a.op_count(), peer_b.op_count());
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn debug_includes_undo_redo_counts() {
         let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(42)).unwrap();
         s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0)).unwrap();
         let d = format!("{s:?}");
         assert!(
             d.contains("undo_count"),
             "Debug must include undo_count: {d}"
         );
         assert!(
             d.contains("redo_count"),
             "Debug must include redo_count: {d}"
         );
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn presence_does_not_dirty_op_log() {
         // Updating presence MUST NOT add ops to the op log
         // (presence is a separate Loro container).
         let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
         let baseline_ops = s.op_count();
         s.update_presence(PresenceState::at_cell(0, 0, 0)).unwrap();
         assert_eq!(
             s.op_count(),
             baseline_ops,
             "update_presence must not append to the op log"
         );
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn peer_id_max_is_rejected_by_constructors() {
         // PeerId(u64::MAX) is Loro's reserved sentinel; both
         // constructors should surface the error.
         let result = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(u64::MAX));
         assert!(
             matches!(result, Err(CollabSessionError::OpLog(_))),
             "new(u64::MAX) must fail; got {result:?}"

     1	//! `Transport` trait — wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold):** trait shape defined.
     4	//! **Phase 5.5 V1 (2026-05-19, `924750819bc`):** `LoopbackTransport`
     5	//! shipped — in-process paired endpoints for 2-peer tests.
     6	//! **Phase 5.5 V2 (pending):** WebSocket impl + reconnect /
     7	//! offline sync semantics.
     8	//!
     9	//! Tests can also use [`NoopTransport`] which discards traffic.
    10	//!
    11	//! The contract is intentionally minimal:
    12	//!
    13	//! - `send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8])` — push bytes to the channel.
    14	//!   Bytes are an opaque blob (a Loro export). The transport
    15	//!   doesn't interpret them.
    16	//! - `try_recv(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` — non-blocking
    17	//!   receive. Returns `None` if no bytes are queued.
    18	//!
    19	//! That's enough to plumb a `CollabSession::export_bytes` →
    20	//! transport → remote `CollabSession::merge_bytes` flow. The
    21	//! transport handles framing / reliability / reconnect / auth as
    22	//! its implementation details.
    23	
    24	use std::collections::VecDeque;
    25	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};
    26	use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
    27	
    28	use thiserror::Error;
    29	
    30	/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
    31	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
    32	#[non_exhaustive]
    33	pub enum TransportError {
    34	    /// The underlying I/O failed (socket dead, network partition,
    35	    /// etc.). Implementations should distinguish recoverable
    36	    /// (reconnect) from permanent (auth rejected) failures via
    37	    /// variant structure in their own error type, then map to this
    38	    /// variant at the trait boundary if they don't want to expose
    39	    /// implementation details.
    40	    #[error("transport I/O error: {0}")]
    41	    Io(String),
    42	
    43	    /// The transport is closed and no further sends are accepted.
    44	    /// Receivers should still drain any already-queued bytes via
    45	    /// `try_recv` before considering the channel done.
    46	    #[error("transport closed")]
    47	    Closed,
    48	}
    49	
    50	/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
    51	///
    52	/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
    53	/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
    54	/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends
    55	/// and the next starts) is the implementation's responsibility.
    56	pub trait Transport {
    57	    /// Push `bytes` to the channel. Implementations may buffer
    58	    /// internally; the call returns when the bytes are
    59	    /// **queued for send**, not when the remote has received them.
    60	    ///
    61	    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent
    62	    /// and re-queue (or surface the error to the user). The
    63	    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives
    64	    /// at the layer above (Phase 5.5 design will pick reconnect
    65	    /// semantics).
    66	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError>;
    67	
    68	    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is
    69	    /// queued, `None` otherwise. Implementations choose how often
    70	    /// they poll the underlying channel; the contract is "if you
    71	    /// have something, give it to me, else `None`."
    72	    ///
    73	    /// Returns `Err(TransportError::Closed)` only when the channel
    74	    /// is permanently closed AND its internal queue is empty. A
    75	    /// transient I/O failure during poll surfaces as
    76	    /// `Err(TransportError::Io)`.
    77	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError>;
    78	}
    79	
    80	/// No-op `Transport` impl for tests + scaffolding.
    81	///
    82	/// Records every `send` call in an internal `Vec` (so tests can
    83	/// assert what would have gone over the wire) and `try_recv`
    84	/// returns `None` forever (no incoming bytes). Suitable for tests
    85	/// that exercise `CollabSession::append_op` without needing a
    86	/// real channel. For 2-peer round-trip tests, use
    87	/// [`LoopbackTransport::pair`] instead — `NoopTransport` is for
    88	/// "I just need a Transport-shaped object" cases.
    89	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
    90	pub struct NoopTransport {
    91	    /// Bytes the consumer would have pushed to the wire. Tests
    92	    /// inspect this to verify their `CollabSession` calls emit the
    93	    /// expected number / shape of exports.
    94	    pub sent: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
    95	    closed: bool,
    96	}
    97	
    98	impl NoopTransport {
    99	    /// Construct a fresh `NoopTransport` with an empty `sent` log
   100	    /// and `closed = false`.
   101	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   102	        Self::default()
   103	    }
   104	
   105	    /// Mark the channel closed. Subsequent `send` calls return
   106	    /// `TransportError::Closed`; `try_recv` returns `Closed` since
   107	    /// nothing's queued.
   108	    pub fn close(&mut self) {
   109	        self.closed = true;
   110	    }
   111	}
   112	
   113	impl Transport for NoopTransport {
   114	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   115	        if self.closed {
   116	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   117	        }
   118	        self.sent.push(bytes.to_vec());
   119	        Ok(())
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
   123	        if self.closed {
   124	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   125	        }
   126	        Ok(None)
   127	    }
   128	}
   129	
   130	/// **Phase 5.5 V1 (2026-05-19):** in-process paired `Transport`
   131	/// impl for 2-peer round-trip testing.
   132	///
   133	/// Constructed via [`LoopbackTransport::pair`]: returns two
   134	/// endpoints `(a, b)` where `a.send(bytes)` lands in `b`'s recv
   135	/// queue and vice versa. The two endpoints share two
   136	/// `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>` channels via interior
   137	/// mutability — `Send + Sync` so they can also be used across
   138	/// threads if a future test wants that.
   139	///
   140	/// ## Use case
   141	///
   142	/// Wire two `CollabSession`s together for tests like:
   143	/// ```ignore
   144	/// let (mut tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
   145	/// let session_a = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1))?;
   146	/// let session_b = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(2))?;
   147	/// // ... append ops on a, drain via tx_a.send / tx_b.try_recv ...
   148	/// ```
   149	///
   150	/// CollabSession does NOT auto-flush to its attached Transport
   151	/// in V1 (Phase 5.5 V2 will wire append → send). For now, tests
   152	/// drive the flow manually: append, export_bytes, send via the
   153	/// transport, recv on the other side, merge_bytes.
   154	///
   155	/// ## Close semantics
   156	///
   157	/// Each endpoint has its OWN `closed` flag (separate
   158	/// `AtomicBool`). `a.close()` shuts down endpoint A:
   159	/// - A's `send` returns `TransportError::Closed` immediately.
   160	/// - A's `try_recv` drains any already-queued bytes FIRST and
   161	///   only returns `Closed` once the queue is empty (per the
   162	///   `Transport` trait contract at the trait docstring).
   163	/// - B is unaffected: B can still `send` (bytes land in A's
   164	///   inbox; A drains them on the next try_recv before reporting
   165	///   Closed) and `try_recv` (drains B's inbox).
   166	///
   167	/// ## Atomic ordering caveat
   168	///
   169	/// `close` uses `AtomicBool` with `Relaxed` ordering. For
   170	/// cross-thread "close then send" semantics, callers must use
   171	/// external synchronization — a thread that observes
   172	/// `is_closed() == true` after another thread's `close()` is NOT
   173	/// guaranteed by this transport alone (the atomic only protects
   174	/// the flag itself, not the surrounding sequence).
   175	pub struct LoopbackTransport {
   176	    /// Channel WE drain via `try_recv`. The peer's `send` writes
   177	    /// here.
   178	    inbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
   179	    /// Channel WE write to via `send`. The peer's `try_recv`
   180	    /// drains here.
   181	    outbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
   182	    /// Per-endpoint close flag. Atomic so `close(&self)` doesn't
   183	    /// need `&mut`.
   184	    closed: AtomicBool,
   185	}
   186	
   187	impl std::fmt::Debug for LoopbackTransport {
   188	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   189	        f.debug_struct("LoopbackTransport")
   190	            .field(
   191	                "inbox_len",
   192	                &self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
   193	            )
   194	            .field(
   195	                "outbox_len",
   196	                &self.outbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
   197	            )
   198	            .field("closed", &self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   199	            .finish()
   200	    }
   201	}
   202	
   203	impl LoopbackTransport {
   204	    /// Construct a pair of `LoopbackTransport` endpoints. Returns
   205	    /// `(a, b)` where `a.send(...)` enqueues to `b.try_recv()` and
   206	    /// `b.send(...)` enqueues to `a.try_recv()`.
   207	    pub fn pair() -> (Self, Self) {
   208	        let a_to_b = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
   209	        let b_to_a = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
   210	        let a = Self {
   211	            inbox: b_to_a.clone(),
   212	            outbox: a_to_b.clone(),
   213	            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
   214	        };
   215	        let b = Self {
   216	            inbox: a_to_b,
   217	            outbox: b_to_a,
   218	            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
   219	        };
   220	        (a, b)
   221	    }
   222	
   223	    /// Mark this endpoint closed. Subsequent `send` / `try_recv`
   224	    /// calls return `TransportError::Closed`. The peer endpoint
   225	    /// is NOT affected by this call (each side has its own close
   226	    /// flag).
   227	    pub fn close(&self) {
   228	        self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   229	    }
   230	
   231	    /// True if this endpoint has been closed.
   232	    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
   233	        self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   234	    }
   235	
   236	    /// Number of bytes blobs waiting in this endpoint's inbox
   237	    /// (i.e. sent by the peer, not yet drained via `try_recv`).
   238	    /// Useful for tests that want to assert "the peer sent N
   239	    /// blobs to me" without consuming them.
   240	    pub fn pending_recv(&self) -> usize {
   241	        self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0)
   242	    }
   243	}
   244	
   245	impl Transport for LoopbackTransport {
   246	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   247	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   248	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   249	        }
   250	        self.outbox
   251	            .lock()
   252	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback outbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?
   253	            .push_back(bytes.to_vec());
   254	        Ok(())
   255	    }
   256	
   257	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
   258	        // Trait contract (transport.rs:71-74): "Closed only when the
   259	        // channel is permanently closed AND its internal queue is
   260	        // empty." So drain any already-queued bytes first; only
   261	        // return Closed when both closed AND drained.
   262	        // Codex + Opus 5.5 V1 audit MEDIUM/HIGH closure: pre-closure
   263	        // we returned Closed immediately on close, violating the
   264	        // contract.
   265	        let mut queue = self
   266	            .inbox
   267	            .lock()
   268	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback inbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?;
   269	        if let Some(bytes) = queue.pop_front() {
   270	            return Ok(Some(bytes));
   271	        }
   272	        // Queue empty — now distinguish "open but empty" from "closed".
   273	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   274	            Err(TransportError::Closed)
   275	        } else {
   276	            Ok(None)
   277	        }
   278	    }
   279	}

   283	// a non-Send/Sync field, this stops compiling.
   284	const _ASSERT_LOOPBACK_TRANSPORT_SEND_SYNC: fn() = || {
   285	    fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>() {}
   286	    assert_send_sync::<LoopbackTransport>();
   287	};

     1	//! `CollabSession` — per-peer collaboration state holder.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** wraps `ql_oplog::OpLog`
     4	//! with peer-id-aware operations + transport plumbing. Subsequent
     5	//! phases populate richer behavior:
     6	//!
     7	//! - ~~**Phase 5.4**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `89c02b9d83e` — per-peer
     8	//!   Loro `UndoManager` tracks local appends. Undo APPENDS
     9	//!   inverse ops + retracts originals from the visible `"ops"`
    10	//!   list (NOT physical removal). Presence-origin commits
    11	//!   excluded. See [`session::CollabSession::undo`].
    12	//! - **Phase 5.5** — transport layer. V1 shipped at
    13	//!   `924750819bc` (LoopbackTransport for in-process 2-peer
    14	//!   tests); V2 pending — WebSocket impl + auto-flush
    15	//!   (append a local op → broadcast bytes via
    16	//!   [`crate::Transport::send`]; poll
    17	//!   [`crate::Transport::try_recv`] for remote bytes →
    18	//!   `merge_bytes`).
    19	//! - ~~**Phase 5.6**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `c677e244704` — `presence`
    20	//!   module + 4 `CollabSession` methods (`update_presence` /
    21	//!   `peer_presence` / `clear_presence` / `peers_with_presence`).
    22	//! - **Phase 5.7** — IDE binding: `CollabSession` becomes the engine
    23	//!   handle that the IDE attaches to each open workbook.
    24	//!
    25	//! What ships in 5.2.a (5.2.b update 2026-05-19):
    26	//!
    27	//! - `CollabSession::new(peer_id)` — fresh session with an empty
    28	//!   `OpLog` and the peer-id wired through to
    29	//!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` (Phase 5.2.b — was a label-only stamp
    30	//!   in 5.2.a). Returns `Result` because Loro's `set_peer_id` is
    31	//!   fallible.
    32	//! - `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` — fork from a
    33	//!   shared base snapshot (used by both peers in the D-4 probe
    34	//!   pattern). The reborn session's peer id is `peer_id`; existing
    35	//!   ops in the imported snapshot retain their original
    36	//!   attribution.
    37	//! - `append_op(&mut self, Op)` — local append; delegates to
    38	//!   `OpLog::append`.
    39	//! - `merge_bytes(&mut self, &[u8])` — pull a remote peer's
    40	//!   snapshot via Loro's CRDT merge; delegates to
    41	//!   `OpLog::merge_bytes`.
    42	//! - `export_bytes(&self)` — produce a snapshot for transport.
    43	//! - `op_log(&self)` — read-only view of the underlying log
    44	//!   (for replay against a workbook).
    45	//! - `peer_id(&self)` — the session's stable peer id.

    47	use thiserror::Error;
    48

    55	/// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
    56	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
    57	#[non_exhaustive]
    58	pub enum CollabSessionError {
    59	    /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
    60	    /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
    61	    #[error("op log error: {0}")]
    62	    OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),
    63	
    64	    /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
    65	    /// underlying OpLog).
    66	    #[error("presence error: {0}")]
    67	    Presence(#[from] PresenceError),
    68	
    69	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
    70	    /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
    71	    /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
    72	    /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
    73	    #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
    74	    Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),
    75	
    76	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 (2026-05-19):** the attached `Transport`
    77	    /// returned an error during `flush_to_transport` or
    78	    /// `poll_remote`. The local `OpLog` state is unaffected — the
    79	    /// caller can retry the flush/poll or detach the transport.
    80	    #[error("transport error: {0}")]
    81	    Transport(#[from] TransportError),
    82	}
    83	
    84	/// Per-peer collaboration state holder.
    85	///
    86	/// Owns an `OpLog` + a stable `PeerId`. Tested with `NoopTransport`
    87	/// at scaffold time; Phase 5.5 will add the live transport
    88	/// integration via `Transport::send` / `try_recv`.
    89	///
    90	/// The session is the engine-side handle for one peer's view of a
    91	/// shared workbook. Multiple peers each hold their own
    92	/// `CollabSession`, exchange byte blobs via [`crate::Transport`],
    93	/// and call `merge_bytes` to incorporate remote ops. All sessions
    94	/// converge to the same `OpLog` content after exchange (per Loro's
    95	/// CRDT determinism — Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1).
    96	pub struct CollabSession {
    97	    peer_id: PeerId,
    98	    log: OpLog,
    99	    /// Phase 5.4 V1 — peer-local undo/redo.
   100	    ///
   101	    /// Drop-order note (Codex 5.4 V1 audit LOW): Rust drops fields
   102	    /// in DECLARATION order, so this drops LAST (after `log`).
   103	    /// That is safe here because `loro::UndoManager` owns its own
   104	    /// `LoroDoc` clone (`loro-internal::undo:166,670`) — it doesn't
   105	    /// borrow from `log`'s doc and won't UAF when `log` drops first.
   106	    undo: loro::UndoManager,
   107	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** optional attached
   108	    /// transport. `None` until `attach_transport` is called.
   109	    /// Boxed + `Send` so callers can move sessions between
   110	    /// threads with attached transports.
   111	    ///
   112	    /// V2 V1 scope: caller drives flush + poll explicitly
   113	    /// (no auto-flush on append). V2 V2 / V3 will add
   114	    /// auto-flush + version-vector delta exports.
   115	    transport: Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>,
   116	}
   117	
   118	impl CollabSession {
   119	    /// Construct a fresh session with an empty op log.
   120	    ///
   121	    /// Use this when no shared base snapshot exists yet (the first
   122	    /// peer to open a brand-new workbook). The `peer_id` is wired
   123	    /// through to the underlying `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so Loro's
   124	    /// CRDT merge metadata attributes this session's appends to
   125	    /// `peer_id` (not Loro's default random per-doc id).
   126	    ///
   127	    /// **Caller pitfalls** (from `OpLog::set_peer_id`):
   128	    /// - Two concurrent sessions MUST use distinct peer ids.
   129	    ///   Duplicate ids corrupt the document via conflicting OpIDs.
   130	    /// - Prefer per-process-random peer ids over user-/device-pinned
   131	    ///   ids unless your transport layer enforces single-ownership.
   132	    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is a Loro-reserved sentinel and returns
   133	    ///   `CollabSessionError::OpLog`.
   134	    pub fn new(peer_id: PeerId) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
   135	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   136	        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
   137	        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
   138	        Ok(Self {
   139	            peer_id,
   140	            log,
   141	            undo,
   142	            transport: None,
   143	        })
   144	    }
   145	
   146	    /// Construct a session by importing a shared snapshot. Use this
   147	    /// when joining a session that already has history (the second+
   148	    /// peer to open a collaborative workbook).
   149	    ///
   150	    /// `bytes` is a `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Snapshot)` produced
   151	    /// by an earlier `CollabSession::export_bytes` call (typically
   152	    /// from the workbook owner / first peer). After import, this
   153	    /// session's peer id is set to `peer_id` — distinct from the
   154	    /// peer ids carried by the already-imported ops. Imported ops
   155	    /// retain their original peer-id attribution (Loro semantics);
   156	    /// only this session's future appends carry `peer_id`.
   157	    ///
   158	    /// **Caller pitfalls** (same as `CollabSession::new`):
   159	    /// - The new `peer_id` MUST differ from every peer already
   160	    ///   represented in the imported snapshot AND from every other
   161	    ///   concurrent session — see `OpLog::set_peer_id` for details.
   162	    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is reserved.
   163	    pub fn from_snapshot(peer_id: PeerId, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
   164	        let mut log = OpLog::import_bytes(bytes)?;
   165	        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
   166	        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
   167	        Ok(Self {
   168	            peer_id,
   169	            log,
   170	            undo,
   171	            transport: None,
   172	        })
   173	    }
   174	
   175	    /// Stable peer id assigned at session creation.
   176	    pub fn peer_id(&self) -> PeerId {
   177	        self.peer_id
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    /// Append a local op. Delegates to `OpLog::append`. Phase 5.5
   181	    /// will additionally push the appended bytes through the
   182	    /// attached `Transport`; 5.2.a leaves transport integration to
   183	    /// the caller.
   184	    pub fn append_op(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
   185	        self.log.append(op)?;
   186	        Ok(())
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /// Merge a remote peer's snapshot into this session's log. Loro's
   190	    /// CRDT preserves both peers' concurrent appends in deterministic
   191	    /// causal order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
   192	    /// Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1). Returns the new `len()` after merge.
   193	    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
   194	        Ok(self.log.merge_bytes(bytes)?)
   195	    }
   196	
   197	    /// Produce a Loro snapshot blob suitable for transport to other
   198	    /// peers OR for `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence.
   199	    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CollabSessionError> {
   200	        Ok(self.log.export_bytes()?)
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    /// Borrow the underlying `OpLog` for replay against a `Workbook`.
   204	    ///
   205	    /// Typical usage:
   206	    /// ```ignore
   207	    /// let session = CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, &bytes)?;
   208	    /// let mut wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();

   210	    /// ql_oplog::replay_into(session.op_log(), &mut wb, &reg)?;
   211	    /// ```
   212	    pub fn op_log(&self) -> &OpLog {
   213	        &self.log
   214	    }
   215	
   216	    /// Current number of ops in the log. Cheap (cached).
   217	    pub fn op_count(&self) -> usize {
   218	        self.log.len()
   219	    }
   220	
   221	    /// True iff the log has no ops (session just opened, no edits yet).
   222	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   223	        self.log.is_empty()
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** attach a `Transport`
   227	    /// implementation to this session. Subsequent
   228	    /// [`flush_to_transport`] calls push the session's current
   229	    /// snapshot through it; [`poll_remote`] drains incoming
   230	    /// bytes and merges them.
   231	    ///
   232	    /// Returns `Some(previous)` if a transport was already

   237	        &mut self,
   238	        transport: T,
   239	    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   240	        self.transport.replace(Box::new(transport))
   241	    }
   242

   244	    /// transport. Returns it for caller cleanup; returns `None`

   247	        self.transport.take()
   248	    }
   249	
   250	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff a transport is

   255	
   256	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** export the current

   260	    ///
   261	    /// Returns `Ok(true)` if bytes were sent. Errors propagate

   373	## Transport (Phase 5.5 — V1 partially shipped)
   374	
   375	**V1 ✅ shipped at `924750819bc` (2026-05-19):** `Transport`
   376	trait + `NoopTransport` (5.2.a) + `LoopbackTransport` (5.5 V1)
   377	— in-process paired endpoints for 2-peer tests. The
   378	`LoopbackTransport::pair()` constructor returns two endpoints
   379	whose sends route to each other's recv queues; `Send + Sync`
   380	so multi-threaded test patterns work too. Drain-before-Closed
   381	semantic per the trait contract.
   382	
   383	Loro's wire format:
   384	- `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Updates(version_vector))` →
   385	  bytes carrying only ops new-to-the-peer-since-version-vector.
   386	- `LoroDoc::import(bytes)` → merge into local doc.
   387	
   388	**V2 pending:** WebSocket transport (or any bidirectional byte
   389	channel) for production. Each peer pushes version-vector
   390	updates to the server; server fan-outs to other peers; each
   391	peer imports. V2 also adds auto-flush wiring on `CollabSession`
   392	(append a local op → push via attached `Transport`). V1's
   393	`LoopbackTransport` is sufficient for engine-side tests.
   394	
   395	5.1 does NOT pick a production transport (WS vs Server-Sent
   396	Events vs custom protocol) — that's 5.5 V2's call. 5.1 just
   397	confirms the substrate works regardless; 5.5 V1 proves it via
   398	LoopbackTransport.

   404	parquet cell data. Op log persistence (`ql-oplog::persistence`)
   405	saves the LoroDoc snapshot as `oplog.bin`.

   300	    /// Export the log to a binary blob suitable for on-disk persistence.
   301	    /// Uses Loro's `ExportMode::Snapshot` — includes full state + history,
   302	    /// compressed.
   303	    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, OpLogError> {
   304	        Ok(self.doc.export(ExportMode::Snapshot)?)
   305	    }
   306	
   307	    /// Reconstruct an `OpLog` from a previously-exported snapshot.
   308	    /// If the snapshot doesn't carry our `"ops"` container shape,
   309	    /// subsequent `iter` / `len` calls operate on an empty list
   310	    /// (Loro creates absent root containers on first access).
   311	    pub fn import_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, OpLogError> {
   312	        let doc = LoroDoc::new();
   313	        doc.import(bytes)?;
   314	        Ok(Self { doc })
   315	    }
   316	
   317	    /// **Phase 5.2 D-4 (2026-05-19):** merge another peer's snapshot
   318	    /// into this log via Loro's CRDT merge. Concurrent appends to
   319	    /// the `"ops"` LoroList are preserved in deterministic causal
   320	    /// order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
   321	    /// `loro-internal::container::richtext::tracker::crdt_rope`).
   322	    ///
   323	    /// Typical pattern for multi-peer collaboration:
   324	    /// 1. Both peers start from a shared snapshot (`import_bytes`).
   325	    /// 2. Each peer appends its own ops independently.
   326	    /// 3. Peer A exports its current state via `export_bytes`.
   327	    /// 4. Peer B calls `merge_bytes(&a_bytes)` — peer B's log now
   328	    ///    contains both peers' ops in causal order.
   329	    /// 5. Replay against a workbook reconstructs the merged state.
   330	    /// 6. `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all` resolves derived state
   331	    ///    (spills, formula values) from the merged final state.
   332	    ///
   333	    /// Returns the new `len()` after merge.
   334	    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, OpLogError> {
   335	        self.doc.import(bytes)?;
   336	        Ok(self.len())
   337	    }

     1	//! `ql-collab` — Phase 5 multi-user CRDT collaboration crate.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** establishes the
     4	//! module shape per the audit-closed design at
     5	//! `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md`. Phase 5.1 locked Option
     6	//! A (preserve op-log shape on top of Loro `LoroList`); this crate
     7	//! is the layer above ql-oplog that orchestrates multi-peer
     8	//! collaboration.
     9	//!
    10	//! ## Module layout
    11	//!
    12	//! - [`peer`] — `PeerId` type. Stable per-session identifier used
    13	//!   to attribute ops + presence state. Wraps Loro's `u64` peer id.
    14	//! - [`session`] — `CollabSession`: per-peer state holder that
    15	//!   wraps an `OpLog`, manages local appends, and exposes
    16	//!   `merge_bytes` + `export_bytes` for transport integration.
    17	//! - [`transport`] — `Transport` trait + 2 impls: `NoopTransport`
    18	//!   (test stub, records sends + returns None forever) and
    19	//!   `LoopbackTransport` (Phase 5.5 V1 ship 2026-05-19 —
    20	//!   in-process paired endpoints for 2-peer round-trip tests).
    21	//!   Phase 5.5 V2 will add a WebSocket impl.
    22	//! - [`presence`] — per-peer ephemeral cursor + selection state
    23	//!   (Phase 5.6 V1 ship 2026-05-19). `PresenceState` JSON-encoded
    24	//!   into the shared `LoroDoc`'s `"presence"` LoroMap (LWW per key).
    25	//! - [`undo`] — peer-local undo/redo via Loro's `UndoManager`
    26	//!   (Phase 5.4 V1 ship 2026-05-19). `CollabSession` wires the
    27	//!   manager + auto-excludes presence-origin commits.
    28	//!
    29	//! ## Stability
    30	//!
    31	//! Pre-0.2.0: the public API is in flux. Phase 5.2.b through 5.7
    32	//! will populate the reserved modules and may shift trait shapes.
    33	//! No `#[non_exhaustive]` on enums yet — locked at Phase 5.8
    34	//! megaudit per the engine audit-discipline rule.
    35	//!
    36	//! ### Pre-stability API changes (logged)
    37	//!
    38	//! - **Phase 5.2.b (2026-05-19, `ef056f50bee`):** `CollabSession::new`
    39	//!   return type changed from `Self` to
    40	//!   `Result<Self, CollabSessionError>`. Loro's `set_peer_id` is
    41	//!   fallible (`u64::MAX` is reserved), so the constructor must
    42	//!   propagate the error. Internal-only at the time of the change;
    43	//!   no external callers.
    44	//! - **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19, `89c02b9d83e` + audit closure):**
    45	//!   `CollabSessionError::Undo(loro::LoroError)` variant added.
    46	//!   Exposes `loro::LoroError` in the public error surface — pre-0.2.0
    47	//!   this couples consumers to the loro crate version; a stable API
    48	//!   would wrap it in a ql-collab-owned error. Will be re-typed
    49	//!   before 0.2.0.
    50	//! - **Phase 5.4 V1 audit closure (2026-05-19):**
    51	//!   `ql_oplog::OpLog::set_peer_id` signature changed from `&self`
    52	//!   to `&mut self`. Prevents accidental peer-id changes through
    53	//!   `CollabSession::op_log()` (`&OpLog`) which would silently clear
    54	//!   an attached `UndoManager`'s stacks per Loro's internal
    55	//!   subscription behavior (`loro-internal::undo:654-662`).
    56	
    57	pub mod peer;
    58	pub mod presence;
    59	pub mod session;
    60	pub mod transport;
    61	pub mod undo;
    62

    64	pub use presence::{PresenceError, PresenceState};
    65	pub use session::{CollabSession, CollabSessionError};
    66	pub use transport::{LoopbackTransport, NoopTransport, Transport, TransportError};
    67	pub use undo::UndoManager;

PASS. `Transport` is object-safe: only `&mut self` methods, no generics, no `Self` returns. `NoopTransport` is `Send` by fields; `LoopbackTransport` is `Send + Sync` and pinned at [transport.rs:284](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:284). This is O(state), but explicitly documented and acceptable for manual wrapper plumbing. Deltas are needed before production/auto-flush.

A3. LOW. `Ok(false)` is workable and documented, but an outcome enum would be clearer.

A4. LOW. Current in-process transports are finite; future live transports should cap drain.

A5. PASS for `flush_to_transport`: export happens before send, and send failure leaves local log unchanged. Do not generalize that statement to `poll_remote`.

**B. API Design**
B1. PASS. `'static` is acceptable because attached transports should own channel state.

B2. PASS for V1. Returning `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` is usable for lifecycle cleanup; concrete downcast can wait unless callers need it.

B3. PASS. Swap semantics are reasonable. A stricter `set_transport` can be added later.

B4. NEEDS cleanup. Propagating `Closed` is acceptable only if the partial-merge semantics are explicit and tested.
